### 17. P5 (29:57-69) Death, Rain, and Returning Life
- Semantic invariant: Life moves through loss, return, descending water, and renewed circulation rather than remaining a self-sustaining possession.
- Surface relation: direct; 29:57 names every self tasting death and returning, while 29:63 joins rain, dead earth, and renewed life.
- Surprising reach: Mortality and revival become a hydraulic cycle of exhausted substrate, delivered water, open channels, organic yield, and restored circulation.

#### Subchannel A. Life-Bearing Self, Death, and Return
- Reading type: mixed
- Scene or process: A living self loses force, passes through a death-state, experiences it directly, and is returned toward its source.
- Active motifs: loss of life and force `quranic:root_001454:B001/m01`; lifeless land or object `quranic:root_001454:B003/m01`; stillness and wearing out `quranic:root_001454:B012/m01`; life-bearing self `quranic:root_001533:B011/m01`; the thing's own identity `quranic:root_001533:B012/m01`; return to an earlier source or condition `quranic:root_000544:B001/m01`; living creature and animated life `quranic:root_000383:B003/m01`; experiential tasting and trial `quranic:root_000526:B002/m01`
- Ayah anchors: م و ت 29:57,63; ن ف س 29:57; ر ج ع 29:57; ح ي ي 29:63-64; ذ و ق 29:57.
- Synthesis: Death takes the force from the very self that constituted living identity, not from an expendable exterior. "Tasting" makes mortality experiential, while return prevents that loss of force from being the route's final direction.

#### Subchannel B. Descending Water and Revived Ground
- Reading type: mixed
- Scene or process: Water is delivered from above, enters exhausted ground, and converts it into a living, productive surface.
- Active motifs: water in its basic substance `quranic:root_001458:B001/m01`; water appearing and increasing in ground or vessel `quranic:root_001458:B002/m01`; watering by delivery and pouring `quranic:root_001458:B003/m01`; rain descending and settling `quranic:root_001492:B001/m01`; water delivered downward `quranic:root_001492:B002/m03`; land revived by rain and vegetation `quranic:root_000383:B002/m01`; soft productive ground `quranic:root_000025:B002/m01`; rain as provision `quranic:root_000560:B003/m01`
- Ayah anchors: م و ه 29:63; ن ز ل 29:63; ح ي ي 29:63-64; ء ر ض 29:61,63; ر ز ق 29:60,62.
- Synthesis: Revival is a material conversion: descending water enters an apparently finished substrate and reopens its productive capacity. The same rain is provision because it feeds a system before it appears as food in a hand.

#### Subchannel C. Directed Channels and Continuous Yield
- Reading type: latent/lexical
- Scene or process: Water is admitted into a measured channel, cuts a course through earth, and sustains continuing growth and allotment.
- Active motifs: directing water into a channel `quranic:root_000009:B004/m01`; emergence of crop, fruit, and abundant water `quranic:root_000009:B007/m01`; river cutting through ground `quranic:root_001559:B001/m01`; opening and widening a course `quranic:root_001559:B003/m01`; flowing water and rivers `quranic:root_000240:B001/m01`; continuing provision or endowment `quranic:root_000240:B006/m01`; palm-heart and new growth `quranic:root_001286:B006/m01`; beneficent condition and gift `quranic:root_001525:B001/m01`
- Ayah anchors: ء ت ي 29:66; ن ه ر 29:58; ج ر ي 29:58; ك ث ر 29:63; ن ع م 29:58,67.
- Synthesis: Water becomes durable life only when routed: admission, incision, flow, and continuing yield form one irrigation process. The promised rivers and divine bounty are thereby linked to an engineered continuity rather than a static abundance.

### 18. P5 (29:57-69) Prepared Shelter and Elevated Abiding
- Semantic invariant: A lasting dwelling combines protective enclosure, prepared placement, vertical refuge, and an uninterrupted life-source.
- Surface relation: direct; 29:58 promises garden chambers with rivers beneath and lasting residence.
- Surprising reach: Paradise is articulated through shield, hidden heart, prepared lodging, upper-story architecture, and water drawn or flowing below.

#### Subchannel A. Enclosed Garden and Settled Inner Life
- Reading type: mixed
- Scene or process: Dense growth encloses a protected place, a resident is established within it, and both dwelling and inward state remain fixed.
- Active motifs: concealment from the senses `quranic:root_000266:B001/m01`; garden enclosed by dense trees `quranic:root_000266:B003/m01`; protective shield `quranic:root_000266:B008/m01`; hidden heart or inward chamber `quranic:root_000266:B010/m01`; durable continuance `quranic:root_000429:B001/m01`; settled attachment to a place `quranic:root_000429:B002/m01`; stable thought in the heart `quranic:root_000429:B004/m01`; prepared emplacement `quranic:root_000161:B001/m01`
- Ayah anchors: ج ن ن 29:58; خ ل د 29:58; ب و ء 29:58.
- Synthesis: The garden's enclosure is simultaneously botanical, defensive, and inward. Abiding means that resident, place, and inner composure cease to pull apart; the prepared dwelling becomes a settled state of the heart.

#### Subchannel B. Upper Chamber Over Flowing Provision
- Reading type: mixed
- Scene or process: A resident is placed in an upper room while water and prepared provision circulate beneath the structure.
- Active motifs: upper room built above another level `quranic:root_001079:B002/m01`; drawing or lifting a measured portion `quranic:root_001079:B001/m01`; lower direction beneath `quranic:root_000177:B001/m01`; river cutting a watercourse `quranic:root_001559:B001/m01`; continuous flow `quranic:root_000240:B001/m01`; prepared provision for a lodger `quranic:root_001492:B005/m01`; agreeable place and pleasant stay `quranic:root_001525:B011/m01`
- Ayah anchors: غ ر ف 29:58; ت ح ت 29:58; ن ه ر 29:58; ج ر ي 29:58; ن ز ل 29:63; ن ع م 29:58,67.
- Synthesis: The image is architectural and hydraulic: elevation protects the resident while flow below continuously supplies the site. The "room" also retains the action of scooping a portion, making lofty placement and measured receipt part of one hospitable design.

### 19. P5 (29:57-69) Provision Without Self-Sufficiency
- Semantic invariant: Sustenance depends on apportioned expansion and reliable custody, not on a creature's ability to carry and secure its own future.
- Surface relation: direct; 29:59-62 joins patience, trust, creatures unable to carry provision, divine provision, and expansion or restriction of allotment.
- Surprising reach: Dependence becomes freight, surety, agency, crawling motion, spread cloth, extended hand, and exact metering.

#### Subchannel A. Load, Guarantor, and Entrusted Care
- Reading type: mixed
- Scene or process: A moving creature lacks the means to bear its store, so responsibility passes to a guarantor or entrusted agent who preserves and supplies it.
- Active motifs: carrying a visible load `quranic:root_000357:B001/m01`; guaranteeing an obligation `quranic:root_000357:B004/m01`; exhausting effort under a load `quranic:root_000357:B007/m01`; creature moving lightly or slowly on earth `quranic:root_000457:B001/m01`; path along which small creatures or water move `quranic:root_000457:B012/m01`; surety and continuing accompaniment `quranic:root_000840:B003/m01`; entrusting an affair to another `quranic:root_001681:B001/m01`; agent who suffices, guards, and manages `quranic:root_001681:B006/m01`
- Ayah anchors: ح م ل 29:60; د ب ب 29:60; ص ب ر 29:59; و ك ل 29:59.
- Synthesis: The creature's inability to carry provision is not inactivity but a mismatch between mobility and storage. Trust names a transfer of care to a competent agent, while patience resembles surety that remains with the dependent party through the interval.

#### Subchannel B. Spreading, Extending, and Exact Constriction
- Reading type: mixed
- Scene or process: An allotter spreads resources or extends a giving hand, then narrows the quantity to an exact measure.
- Active motifs: spreading and extension `quranic:root_000116:B001/m01`; broad level ground or carpet `quranic:root_000116:B002/m01`; increase and abundance `quranic:root_000116:B003/m01`; extending the hand to give or withhold `quranic:root_000116:B004/m01`; measured quantity and limit `quranic:root_001205:B001/m01`; constricting provision `quranic:root_001205:B004/m01`; planning an allotment by measure `quranic:root_001205:B005/m01`; assigned gift or share `quranic:root_000560:B001/m01`
- Ayah anchors: ب س ط 29:62; ق د ر 29:62; ر ز ق 29:60,62.
- Synthesis: Expansion and constriction are two calibrated gestures of one allocator. The spread ground and extended hand make abundance spatial, while قدر returns it to a bounded quantity suited to the recipient and time.

### 20. P5 (29:57-69) Maritime Distress and Reversed Loyalty
- Semantic invariant: Loss of control at sea purifies dependence to one addressee, but restored footing permits divided allegiance to reappear.
- Surface relation: direct; 29:65-66 moves from boarding ship, sincere invocation, rescue to land, renewed association, and denial of what was given.
- Surprising reach: The scene joins mounting, unstable waves, extraction from a snare, purified mixture, dry ground, partnership, and repudiation.

#### Subchannel A. Unstable Vessel and the Purified Call
- Reading type: mixed
- Scene or process: Passengers mount a vessel, waves destabilize its course, entanglement strips away alternatives, and a single unalloyed call is directed outward.
- Active motifs: mounting a vessel or conveyance `quranic:root_000589:B001/m01`; layers riding one above another `quranic:root_000589:B002/m01`; vessel moving on water `quranic:root_001177:B003/m01`; wave moving back and forth `quranic:root_001177:B007/m01`; vocal summons that inclines an addressee `quranic:root_000478:B001/m01`; obedience and religious submission `quranic:root_000504:B001/m01`; purification after mixture `quranic:root_000430:B001/m01`; escape from entanglement `quranic:root_000430:B002/m01`; exclusive devotion without partnership `quranic:root_000430:B004/m01`
- Ayah anchors: ر ك ب 29:65; ف ل ك 29:65; د ع و 29:65; د ي ن 29:65; خ ل ص 29:65.
- Synthesis: Sea danger removes the illusion of stable mounting: vessel, wave, and passenger are all layers riding unstable layers. إخلاص is a filtering operation in which mixed allegiances fall away until one addressee remains.

#### Subchannel B. Dry-Ground Rescue and Renewed Division
- Reading type: mixed
- Scene or process: Extraction reaches safe dry land; once the snare is gone, allegiance is repartitioned and the received benefit is disowned.
- Active motifs: escape by separation `quranic:root_001476:B001/m01`; raised ground beyond inundation `quranic:root_001476:B004/m01`; dry land opposed to sea `quranic:root_000104:B005/m01`; shared ownership or partnership `quranic:root_000791:B001/m01`; assigning a partner in worship `quranic:root_000791:B002/m01`; divided inward opinion `quranic:root_000791:B008/m01`; denial of a received benefit `quranic:root_001307:B004/m01`; disavowal after attachment `quranic:root_001307:B005/m01`
- Ayah anchors: ن ج و 29:65; ب ر ر 29:65; ش ر ك 29:65; ك ف ر 29:66-68.
- Synthesis: Rescue is a movement from unstable water to raised, dry footing. Precisely when the environment becomes divisible and controllable again, the previously purified relation is divided into shares and the saving benefit is repudiated.

### 21. P5 (29:57-69) Sanctuary as Protected Perimeter
- Semantic invariant: Security is produced by a bounded interior and is exposed by the speed with which predatory taking operates outside it.
- Surface relation: direct; 29:67 contrasts a made secure sanctuary with people being snatched from its surroundings.
- Surprising reach: Sacred security becomes perimeter law, buffer zone, obstacle, hooked extraction, and the difference between trusted stillness and high-speed capture.

#### Subchannel A. Bounded Interior, Buffer, and Protected Right
- Reading type: mixed
- Scene or process: A protected center is established, surrounded by a legally meaningful buffer, and held apart from external access.
- Active motifs: prohibition that blocks violation `quranic:root_000313:B001/m01`; sacred precinct and spatial buffer `quranic:root_000313:B003/m01`; protected right and inviolable bond `quranic:root_000313:B006/m01`; inward security and trust `quranic:root_000054:B001/m01`; surrounding direction `quranic:root_000373:B004/m01`; barrier separating two sides `quranic:root_000373:B005/m01`; placing something into a protected state `quranic:root_000248:B002/m01`
- Ayah anchors: ح ر م 29:67; ء م ن 29:58,67; ح و ل 29:67; ج ع ل 29:67.
- Synthesis: The sanctuary is not merely a point but a center-plus-buffer system. Prohibition, right, surrounding zone, and separating barrier make security spatially and normatively produced.

#### Subchannel B. Hooked Snatching Beyond the Boundary
- Reading type: mixed
- Scene or process: Outside the protected interior, a fast taker uses sudden grip or hook to extract exposed persons before stable response is possible.
- Active motifs: rapid seizure and stripping `quranic:root_000423:B001/m01`; hooked iron or claw `quranic:root_000423:B002/m01`; predatory or human snatcher `quranic:root_000423:B003/m01`; neck-drawn speed in flight `quranic:root_000423:B004/m01`; exposed surrounding zone `quranic:root_000373:B004/m01`; direct visual apprehension `quranic:root_000531:B001/m01`; land as exposed supporting field `quranic:root_000025:B001/m01`
- Ayah anchors: خ ط ف 29:67; ح و ل 29:67; ر ء ي 29:67; ء ر ض 29:61,63.
- Synthesis: The outside is defined by extraction speed: grip, hook, predator, and accelerated withdrawal leave little time for collective defense. The verse's contrast thus measures sanctuary by the predatory mechanics it suspends.

### 22. P5 (29:57-69) Fabricated Appearance Against Fitted Truth
- Semantic invariant: Falsehood is made by cutting, coating, or turning material away from its proper fit; truth is what remains fixed and correctly joined.
- Surface relation: direct; 29:61 speaks of being turned away, 29:67 contrasts falsehood with blessing, and 29:68 joins fabrication, lie, denial, and truth's arrival.
- Surprising reach: Lying becomes leatherwork, destructive cutting, counterfeit plating, false-colored cloth, and a misaligned joint.

#### Subchannel A. Cutting, Stitching, and Counterfeit Surface
- Reading type: latent/lexical
- Scene or process: A maker cuts material, either repairs or damages it, then coats or colors the surface so that the finished object presents a false condition.
- Active motifs: cutting leather for repair and stitching `quranic:root_001150:B001/m01`; destructive splitting and cutting `quranic:root_001150:B002/m01`; fabricating a lie `quranic:root_001150:B003/m01`; plating base metal with gold or silver `quranic:root_001458:B005/m01`; inventing manufactured speech `quranic:root_000434:B007/m01`; false-colored patterned cloth `quranic:root_001290:B009/m01`; lie diverted from truth `quranic:root_000041:B002/m01`
- Ayah anchors: ف ر ي 29:68; م و ه 29:63; خ ل ق 29:61; ك ذ ب 29:68; ء ف ك 29:61.
- Synthesis: Fabrication is craft with an ethical fork: cutting can prepare repair, or it can rupture; coating can protect, or it can counterfeit. The false product succeeds by making surface appearance deny underlying material.

#### Subchannel B. Turning Away From the Proper Fit
- Reading type: mixed
- Scene or process: A claim is rotated away from its correct orientation, while truth is tested by whether assertion, evidence, and fitting place coincide.
- Active motifs: turning a thing from its proper direction `quranic:root_000041:B001/m01`; weakened judgment after diversion `quranic:root_000041:B006/m01`; truth fixed to reality `quranic:root_000347:B001/m01`; proof that manifests truth `quranic:root_000347:B005/m01`; part fitting its proper joint or receptacle `quranic:root_000347:B011/m01`; falsehood without stability `quranic:root_000127:B001/m01`; false assertion `quranic:root_000127:B003/m01`; contradiction of truth in speech or act `quranic:root_001290:B001/m01`
- Ayah anchors: ء ف ك 29:61; ح ق ق 29:68; ب ط ل 29:67; ك ذ ب 29:68.
- Synthesis: Falsehood is directional failure: statement and evidence are turned away from the place where they should meet. Truth is the opposite operation, a stable match like a part seated in its proper joint.

### 23. P5 (29:57-69) Striving Through Difficult Terrain
- Semantic invariant: Sustained exertion makes a route legible and traversable, while guidance supplies direction and support proportionate to the traveler's difficulty.
- Surface relation: direct; 29:69 joins striving, guidance, multiple paths, and excellent conduct.
- Surprising reach: Guidance appears as exposed track, advance party, bodily support, hard ground, mountain center, and a gait stabilized against collapse.

#### Subchannel A. Exertion Until the Route Appears
- Reading type: mixed
- Scene or process: A traveler spends capacity under pressure until the road stands out, then receives direction into its viable courses.
- Active motifs: expending capacity under hardship `quranic:root_000268:B001/m01`; being driven to the edge of capacity `quranic:root_000268:B003/m01`; road or truth becoming exposed `quranic:root_000268:B007/m01`; extended route to a destination `quranic:root_000672:B001/m01`; travelers using the road `quranic:root_000672:B002/m01`; gentle direction to path and truth `quranic:root_001583:B001/m01`; orientation and manner of proceeding `quranic:root_001583:B002/m01`; excellent action `quranic:root_000323:B002/m01`
- Ayah anchors: ج ه د 29:69; س ب ل 29:69; ه د ي 29:69; ح س ن 29:69.
- Synthesis: Striving does not purchase guidance but brings the traveler to the point where a route can be shown as route. The plural paths are viable courses disclosed after exertion has exposed need, direction, and capacity.

#### Subchannel B. Hard Ground, Mountain Center, and Supported Gait
- Reading type: latent/lexical
- Scene or process: A weakened traveler crosses bare hard terrain, uses a stable central landmark, and advances with bodily support rather than uncontrolled haste.
- Active motifs: bare hard ground `quranic:root_000268:B004/m01`; stony terrain `quranic:root_000840:B005/m01`; mountain and its stable middle `quranic:root_000840:B017/m01`; route and means of access `quranic:root_000672:B001/m01`; assisted swaying gait `quranic:root_001583:B008/m01`; calm and well-formed movement `quranic:root_001583:B010/m01`; lower terrestrial surface `quranic:root_000025:B001/m01`
- Ayah anchors: ج ه د 29:69; ص ب ر 29:59; س ب ل 29:69; ه د ي 29:69; ء ر ض 29:61,63.
- Synthesis: The latent travel scene refuses a frictionless idea of guidance. Hard ground and stony ascent remain real; guidance supplies a stable middle, an assisted gait, and a calm manner of proceeding through them.

### 24. P5 (29:57-69) Worldly Life as Game and Distraction
- Semantic invariant: Immediate play diverts attention from a deferred reality and consumes or risks what should sustain purposeful life.
- Surface relation: direct; 29:64 names the present life as play and distraction and the later dwelling as life in the fullest sense.
- Surprising reach: Diversion becomes aimless performance, wind-erased tracks, wager, mill-feed, and the exchange of a deferred outcome for an immediate stake.

#### Subchannel A. Aimless Performance and Diverted Attention
- Reading type: mixed
- Scene or process: An activity expends motion without a sound end while another attraction turns attention away from the lasting object.
- Active motifs: play lacking a valid aim `quranic:root_001358:B001/m01`; wind playing through and erasing a place `quranic:root_001358:B005/m01`; occupation that turns one away from another object `quranic:root_001382:B001/m01`; amusement and enjoyed diversion `quranic:root_001382:B002/m01`; the near and lower life `quranic:root_000493:B002/m01`; what comes after the first `quranic:root_000019:B001/m01`; useless inactivity `quranic:root_000127:B005/m01`
- Ayah anchors: ل ع ب 29:64; ل ه و 29:64; د ن و 29:64; ء خ ر 29:64; ب ط ل 29:67.
- Synthesis: Worldly play is not condemned because it moves, but because its motion has no stable end and is easily erased like tracks under wind. Distraction compounds the loss by turning attention from the later reality that gives action an enduring object.

#### Subchannel B. Wagering the Immediate Against the Deferred
- Reading type: latent/lexical
- Scene or process: A player stakes an immediate possession, feeds resources into a game-like mechanism, and risks the deferred return for a near gain.
- Active motifs: gambling and victory by wager or deception `quranic:root_001255:B007/m01`; game or plaything `quranic:root_001358:B001/m02`; professional player `quranic:root_001358:B001/m03`; grain cast into a mill-mouth and gift likened to it `quranic:root_001382:B003/m01`; nourishment consumed `quranic:root_000560:B002/m01`; deferral to a later time `quranic:root_000019:B002/m01`; baseness of the lower choice `quranic:root_000493:B003/m01`
- Ayah anchors: ق م ر 29:61; ل ع ب 29:64; ل ه و 29:64; ر ز ق 29:60,62; ء خ ر 29:64; د ن و 29:64.
- Synthesis: The present life becomes a wager when sustenance is fed into an immediate contest whose apparent gain is produced by postponing the real settlement. The lunar root's gambling sense makes the created moon itself a pressure on treating the near world as a gaming table.

## Standalone Subchannels

### S1. P1 (29:1-13) Coerced Kinship and Voluntary Obedience
- Reading type: mixed
- Scene or process: Parents press a child toward a shared allegiance, while the child distinguishes inherited bond from willing conformity.
- Active motifs: parents as the pair from whom birth proceeds `quranic:root_001683:B002/m01`; a connected injunction entrusted to another `quranic:root_001656:B002/m01`; reciprocal exhortation within a group `quranic:root_001656:B003/m01`; forcing a person to the edge of capacity `quranic:root_000268:B003/m01`; obedience and tractable movement `quranic:root_000956:B001/m01`; agreement between two parties `quranic:root_000956:B002/m01`; the self making an act seem easy `quranic:root_000956:B006/m01`; shared ownership or participation `quranic:root_000791:B001/m01`; assigning a partner in worship `quranic:root_000791:B002/m01`
- Ayah anchors: و ل د 29:8; و ص ي 29:8; ج ه د 29:6,8; ط و ع 29:8; ش ر ك 29:8.
- Synthesis: The parental injunction is a connected bond, but its coercive extension tries to convert relation into agreement and shared cultic ownership. The scene distinguishes honoring the birth-bond from letting pressure make the self facilitate partnership.

### S2. P1 (29:1-13) Hypocrisy as a Hidden Tunnel
- Reading type: latent/lexical
- Scene or process: A person enters through a visible opening, conceals a second outlet, and uses the passage to display one affiliation while escaping through another.
- Active motifs: underground passage with an outlet `quranic:root_001537:B003/m01`; displaying an entrance while concealing an exit `quranic:root_001537:B004/m01`; hidden interior and secret disposition `quranic:root_000464:B003/m01`; concealed corruption or deception `quranic:root_000464:B004/m01`; outsider inserted among a group `quranic:root_000464:B005/m01`; physical covering `quranic:root_001307:B001/m01`; concealment and denial of truth `quranic:root_001307:B003/m01`
- Ayah anchors: ن ف ق 29:11; د خ ل 29:9; ك ف ر 29:7,12.
- Synthesis: Hypocrisy is spatialized as a two-mouthed tunnel: public entry is visible, private exit is hidden, and concealed corruption lets an inserted member change sides without crossing an exposed threshold. The image gives structural form to profession without durable belonging.

### S3. P1 (29:1-13) Moral Repair and Re-entry
- Reading type: mixed
- Scene or process: A fault is covered or removed, the damaged thing is restored to fitness, and it is admitted into a sound assembly.
- Active motifs: covering an offense until its effect is erased `quranic:root_001307:B009/m01`; ugliness and defect `quranic:root_000755:B001/m01`; identifying and censuring a fault `quranic:root_000755:B005/m01`; beneficent repair beyond bare justice `quranic:root_000323:B002/m01`; restoration opposed to corruption `quranic:root_000876:B001/m01`; fitness and compatibility `quranic:root_000876:B003/m01`; entry into an interior `quranic:root_000464:B001/m01`; parts interlocking within a body or structure `quranic:root_000464:B008/m01`
- Ayah anchors: ك ف ر 29:7,12; س و ء 29:4,7; ح س ن 29:7-8; ص ل ح 29:7,9; د خ ل 29:9.
- Synthesis: The movement from erased wrongs to entry among the صالحين is a repair-and-fit sequence. Covering removes the operative defect, reform restores compatibility, and entry seats the repaired part within a sound whole.

### S4. P1 (29:1-13) Exertion Spent on the Self
- Reading type: mixed
- Scene or process: Effort consumes the worker's own capacity and material, while the beneficiary who needs nothing remains outside that economy of depletion.
- Active motifs: spending full capacity under hardship `quranic:root_000268:B001/m01`; exhausting a substance through use or extraction `quranic:root_000268:B005/m01`; the thing itself in its full identity `quranic:root_001533:B012/m01`; independence from need `quranic:root_001110:B001/m01`; sufficiency that stands in for another `quranic:root_001110:B002/m01`; labor undertaken with strain `quranic:root_001046:B007/m01`
- Ayah anchors: ج ه د 29:6,8; ن ف س 29:6; غ ن ي 29:6; ع م ل 29:4,7-9.
- Synthesis: Striving is self-consuming in the literal sense: capacity and material are spent in the worker's own process. Divine independence breaks any imagined exchange in which that depletion enriches God; its transformation returns to the striving self.

### S5. P2 (29:14-27) The Migrant as Stranger in Habitable Terrain
- Reading type: mixed
- Scene or process: A person leaves a first dwelling, crosses exposed or hot terrain with a traveling group, and arrives among people to whom he is initially foreign.
- Active motifs: separation and departure `quranic:root_001578:B001/m01`; relocation from one dwelling to another `quranic:root_001578:B002/m01`; travel in intense noon heat `quranic:root_001578:B005/m01`; movement through land `quranic:root_000769:B001/m01`; forced removal from a country `quranic:root_000769:B005/m01`; traveling caravan `quranic:root_000769:B006/m01`; foreigner called a son of the earth `quranic:root_000025:B004/m01`; delayed residence on the ground `quranic:root_000025:B006/m02`; outsider entering another people `quranic:root_000009:B006/m01`; traveled road and terminus `quranic:root_000009:B010/m01`; clear road arriving from another country `quranic:root_001464:B007/m01`
- Ayah anchors: ه ج ر 29:26; س ي ر 29:20; ء ر ض 29:20,22; ء ت ي 29:27; ن ب ء 29:27.
- Synthesis: Abraham's migration becomes a complete relocation scene: severance, exposure, caravan movement, foreign arrival, and eventual orientation by a clear road. "Son of the earth" usefully pressures migration as the remaking of belonging outside inherited territory.

### S6. P2 (29:14-27) Fire Attack and Extractive Rescue
- Reading type: mixed
- Scene or process: A community turns fire into a weapon and a vessel of attack, exposes a target to death, and is answered by an extraction that strips the target free.
- Active motifs: consuming flame `quranic:root_000311:B002/m01`; fire-bearing attack vessel or implement `quranic:root_000311:B008/m01`; blazing fire and its brand `quranic:root_001564:B002/m01`; taking life `quranic:root_001200:B001/m01`; making a person exposed to killing `quranic:root_001200:B005/m01`; separation from danger `quranic:root_001476:B001/m01`; stripping material free from an attached surface `quranic:root_001476:B003/m01`; manifest sign `quranic:root_000074:B003/m01`
- Ayah anchors: ح ر ق 29:24; ن و ر 29:24-25; ق ت ل 29:24; ن ج و 29:15,24; ء ي ي 29:15,23-24.
- Synthesis: The command to burn is not generic violence but a conversion of fire into a delivery technology aimed at making the victim killable. Rescue answers with an opposite material operation: separation and stripping free, leaving the failed attack as a visible sign.

### S7. P2 (29:14-27) Slow Stay and Accelerated Motion
- Reading type: latent/lexical
- Scene or process: A long residence is measured against roots that encode delay, rapid movement, and intervals whose apparent sameness hides different rates.
- Active motifs: staying in one place `quranic:root_001339:B001/m01`; delay and slowness `quranic:root_001339:B002/m01`; year as measured residence `quranic:root_000751:B008/m01`; annual cycle `quranic:root_001063:B002/m01`; interval between years `quranic:root_001063:B010/m01`; a community as a span of time `quranic:root_000053:B008/m01`; very brief stay and rapid running `quranic:root_000973:B009/m01`
- Ayah anchors: ل ب ث 29:14; س ن و 29:14; ع و م 29:14; ء م م 29:18; ع ب د 29:16-17.
- Synthesis: Noah's span is textured by opposed rates: residence, delay, annual turning, inter-year interval, and sudden acceleration. The contrast prevents the large number from remaining a bare count; duration is lived as persistence against the possibility of rapid abandonment.

### S8. P3 (29:28-44) Guests, Provision, and Extraction
- Reading type: mixed
- Scene or process: Arriving messengers are received as guests, provision is prepared, household status is negotiated, and the visitors become agents of extraction.
- Active motifs: dispatch and release `quranic:root_000563:B001/m01`; messenger carrying a report `quranic:root_000563:B002/m01`; prepared food and lodging for a guest `quranic:root_001492:B005/m01`; hospitality through gathered food `quranic:root_001222:B003/m01`; greeting of breadth and familiarity `quranic:root_000064:B005/m01`; glad report that opens the face `quranic:root_000120:B005/m01`; first signs heralding an arrival `quranic:root_000120:B007/m01`; rescue by separation `quranic:root_001476:B001/m01`
- Ayah anchors: ر س ل 29:31,33,40; ن ز ل 29:34; ق ر ي 29:31,34; ء ه ل 29:31-34; ب ش ر 29:31; ن ج و 29:32-33.
- Synthesis: The messengers occupy two roles in one hospitality frame: arrivals who should receive protection and bearers who will remove others from danger. The threatened welcome of the guest-house is reversed when the guests themselves secure the household's extraction.

### S9. P3 (29:28-44) Constricted Reach Opened by Rescue
- Reading type: mixed
- Scene or process: Fear and grief constrict the chest and reduce effective reach until a rescuing arrival creates an exit beyond the person's own capacity.
- Active motifs: extended arm and operative reach `quranic:root_000512:B001/m01`; capacity and limit of reach `quranic:root_000512:B004/m01`; constriction of the chest `quranic:root_000925:B002/m01`; fear anticipating harm `quranic:root_000447:B001/m01`; grief as inward roughness `quranic:root_000317:B001/m01`; glad report changing bodily appearance `quranic:root_000120:B005/m01`; escape by separation `quranic:root_001476:B001/m01`; rapid departure `quranic:root_001476:B002/m01`
- Ayah anchors: ذ ر ع 29:33; ض ي ق 29:33; خ و ف 29:33; ح ز ن 29:33; ب ش ر 29:31; ن ج و 29:32-33.
- Synthesis: "Narrowed in reach" is anatomically exact: the chest constricts and the arm's effective range contracts. Rescue does not deny that incapacity; it supplies an external route whose arrival changes fear into a body capable of departure.

### S10. P3 (29:28-44) Masculinity Severed From Muruwwa
- Reading type: mixed
- Scene or process: Male status is publicly claimed while conduct exceeds moral bounds, severs social bonds, and contradicts the excellence conventionally attached to manhood.
- Active motifs: adult male `quranic:root_000546:B002/m01`; mature humanity and manly excellence `quranic:root_001410:B002/m01`; conspicuous moral ugliness `quranic:root_001134:B001/m01`; exceeding due measure `quranic:root_001134:B002/m01`; obscene speech and conduct `quranic:root_001134:B003/m01`; cutting kinship and connection `quranic:root_001240:B007/m01`; public council `quranic:root_001486:B001/m01`
- Ayah anchors: ر ج ل 29:29; م ر ء 29:32-33; ف ح ش 29:28; ق ط ع 29:29; ن د و 29:29.
- Synthesis: The lexical contrast separates biological maleness from muruwwa, the cultivated completeness of human and social character. Public performance with men does not establish manly excellence when the action itself exceeds bounds and cuts the relations that excellence should protect.

### S11. P3 (29:28-44) Adorned Deeds Blocking a Visible Road
- Reading type: mixed
- Scene or process: Conduct is cosmetically improved, the beautified surface captures judgment, and a barrier redirects even observers who retain visual capacity.
- Active motifs: making a thing appear beautiful `quranic:root_000660:B002/m01`; detachable ornament and display `quranic:root_000660:B003/m01`; intentional deed `quranic:root_001046:B001/m01`; turning a traveler away `quranic:root_000848:B001/m01`; route leading toward water `quranic:root_000848:B004/m01`; ordinary eyesight `quranic:root_000121:B001/m01`; inward evidentiary sight `quranic:root_000121:B002/m01`; distance and severance `quranic:root_000796:B001/m01`; rebellious agent `quranic:root_000796:B004/m01`; arrogant refusal of truth `quranic:root_001281:B006/m01`
- Ayah anchors: ز ي ن 29:38; ع م ل 29:38; ص د د 29:38; ب ص ر 29:38; ش ط ن 29:38; ك ب ر 29:39.
- Synthesis: The obstruction works by presentation before force: deeds receive an attractive surface, and that display recruits sight into misdirection. Remaining "perceptive" is therefore compatible with blocked insight when ornament controls what vision treats as a route.

### S12. P4 (29:45-56) Provenance by Line, Hand, and False Attribution
- Reading type: mixed
- Scene or process: Authorship is tested through written trace, the hand that could make it, oath-like authorization, and the possibility of attaching words to the wrong speaker.
- Active motifs: written line and pen-trace `quranic:root_000422:B001/m01`; divinatory lines claiming to disclose an outcome `quranic:root_000422:B002/m01`; right hand and side of action `quranic:root_001698:B002/m01`; oath and pledged word `quranic:root_001698:B003/m01`; hand as power and authorization `quranic:root_001698:B004/m01`; saying falsely that another spoke `quranic:root_000186:B009/m01`; attributing an unspoken claim `quranic:root_001272:B005/m01`; asserting what has no reality `quranic:root_000127:B003/m01`; destabilizing doubt `quranic:root_000616:B001/m01`
- Ayah anchors: خ ط ط 29:48; ي م ن 29:48; ت ل و 29:45,48,51; ق و ل 29:46,50,52,55; ب ط ل 29:48,52; ر ي ب 29:48.
- Synthesis: The absence of prior writing and right-hand inscription is evidence about provenance, not a dismissal of writing itself. Line, hand, oath, and attribution form a forensic scene in which a false claimant would need both a trace and the authority to attach it to a speaker.

### S13. P4 (29:45-56) Sufficiency That Ends Further Demand
- Reading type: mixed
- Scene or process: A supplied object fills the operative need, stops additional seeking, and remains available as nourishment and reminder.
- Active motifs: managing an affair until need is met `quranic:root_001310:B001/m01`; declaring an object enough `quranic:root_001310:B002/m01`; provisions sufficient for need `quranic:root_001310:B003/m01`; sufficiency that ends pursuit `quranic:root_001560:B005/m01`; written document `quranic:root_001283:B002/m01`; reminder restoring presence `quranic:root_000516:B009/m01`; mercy as active beneficence `quranic:root_000552:B001/m01`
- Ayah anchors: ك ف ي 29:51-52; ن ه ي 29:45; ك ت ب 29:45-48,51; ذ ك ر 29:45,51; ر ح م 29:51.
- Synthesis: The book is "enough" because it continues to manage the need: it nourishes understanding, recalls what fades, and ends the search for a substitute sign. Mercy names the quality of a sufficiency that remains usable rather than merely terminating a request.

### S14. P4 (29:45-56) Spacious Earth as Ground for Service
- Reading type: mixed
- Scene or process: A constrained servant is invited onto broad terrain where capacity, stride, welcome, and worship can be re-established.
- Active motifs: earth as supporting terrain `quranic:root_000025:B001/m01`; productive habitable ground `quranic:root_000025:B002/m01`; broad carpet-like land `quranic:root_000025:B005/m01`; spatial breadth `quranic:root_001647:B001/m01`; capacity to bear action `quranic:root_001647:B002/m01`; practical release from constriction `quranic:root_001647:B005/m01`; broad stride in travel `quranic:root_001647:B006/m01`; submissive worship `quranic:root_000973:B003/m01`; roads dispersing in many directions `quranic:root_000973:B010/m01`; welcome into familiarity rather than foreignness `quranic:root_000064:B005/m01`
- Ayah anchors: ء ر ض 29:52,56; و س ع 29:56; ع ب د 29:56; ء ه ل 29:46.
- Synthesis: The earth's breadth is functional: it restores capacity to move, stride, and serve when one location has become constrictive. The dispersed-road sense of عبادة prevents service from being tied to a single coercive place, while welcome anticipates a new habitable relation.

### S15. P5 (29:57-69) Ordered Celestial Circuits
- Reading type: mixed
- Scene or process: Measured celestial bodies move in rounded tracks under imposed service, making repeated motion evidence of prior design rather than autonomous wandering.
- Active motifs: circular rotation `quranic:root_001177:B001/m01`; celestial track for sun, moon, and stars `quranic:root_001177:B002/m01`; sun and its light `quranic:root_000818:B001/m01`; moon and its light `quranic:root_001255:B001/m01`; abundance of water and pasture under the lunar root `quranic:root_001255:B008/m01`; directing a thing into service `quranic:root_000685:B001/m01`; measuring before making `quranic:root_000434:B001/m01`; originating creation `quranic:root_000434:B002/m01`; completed and balanced form `quranic:root_000434:B003/m01`; preparing and fitting a form `quranic:root_001205:B005/m02`; calculating lunar or numeric cycles `quranic:root_001205:B005/m03`
- Ayah anchors: ف ل ك 29:65; ش م س 29:61; ق م ر 29:61; س خ ر 29:61; خ ل ق 29:61; ق د ر 29:62.
- Synthesis: The sun and moon are not merely luminous objects but components moving through a rounded, measured system. Their repeated circuits are service under design, while the moon's water-and-pasture sense connects celestial ordering to the provision system below.

### S16. Whole-surah (29:1-69) Moonlit Snare Hunt
- Reading type: latent/lexical
- Scene or process: Hunters leave under moonlight, approach behind a screen, set a snare, immobilize the quarry, and inspect whether the captured animal has died.
- Active motifs: setting out to hunt `quranic:root_000745:B006/m01`; hunting birds or gazelles by moonlight `quranic:root_001255:B003/m01`; hunter's entangling snare `quranic:root_000791:B006/m01`; trap set for game `quranic:root_000879:B004/m01`; animal used as a hunter's screen `quranic:root_000512:B005/m01`; immobilization by binding the legs `quranic:root_001036:B010/m01`; checking whether struck quarry has died `quranic:root_001454:B014/m01`
- Ayah anchors: س م و 29:22,34,44,52-53,61,63; ق م ر 29:61; ش ر ك 29:8,65; ص ل و 29:45; ذ ر ع 29:33; ع ق ل 29:35,43,63; م و ت 29:57,63.
- Synthesis: False association becomes capture rather than an abstract mistake: concealment and approach end in entanglement, lost movement, and death. This concretely pressures the surah's contrast between shirk and prayer: the lexical field of شرك supplies the hunter's snare, while a lexical fringe of the prayer root supplies a set trap, so restraint must be judged by its outcome. Prayer restrains indecency while preserving moral agency; false allegiance immobilizes its captive.

### S17. Whole-surah (29:1-69) Childbirth and Postpartum Recovery
- Reading type: latent/lexical
- Scene or process: Labor approaches, a midwife receives the emerging infant, birth separates child from mother, and blood, afterbirth, and uterine pain remain as the body recovers.
- Active motifs: pregnancy nearing delivery `quranic:root_000548:B005/m01`; midwife receiving the newborn into her hands `quranic:root_001198:B007/m01`; childbirth and release of the pregnancy `quranic:root_001683:B003/m01`; birth with postpartum blood `quranic:root_001533:B005/m01`; membrane or afterbirth emerging with the infant `quranic:root_000373:B008/m01`; uterine pain after delivery `quranic:root_000552:B004/m01`
- Ayah anchors: ر ج و 29:5,36; ق ب ل 29:3,18,48; و ل د 29:8; ن ف س 29:6,40,57; ح و ل 29:67; ر ح م 29:21,23,51.
- Synthesis: Generational continuity is embodied labor, not an effortless extension of a name: expectation reaches a threshold, another person receives the new life, and separation leaves blood, membrane, and pain behind. This materializes the surah's parental obligation and gifted descendants by pressing its primary creation-and-continuity reading through the bodily cost of emergence.

### S18. Whole-surah (29:1-69) Timed Watering and Milk Yield
- Reading type: latent/lexical
- Scene or process: A herder cycles stock between pasture and water, waters them at the mouth, returns a thirsty animal to the trough, and regulates drinking and milking by intervals that determine the yield.
- Active motifs: moving camels or horses between water and pasture `quranic:root_001486:B006/m01`; drawing water at the camels' mouths `quranic:root_001198:B014/m01`; returning thirsty camels to drink again `quranic:root_000464:B007/m01`; taking milk or drink in timed portions `quranic:root_001188:B007/m01`; watering or milking at the wrong time or place `quranic:root_000967:B004/m01`; milk refilling between milkings `quranic:root_001188:B006/m01`; milk failing after seeming durable `quranic:root_001290:B006/m01`; increasing milk and offspring in a flock `quranic:root_001694:B006/m01`
- Ayah anchors: ن د و 29:29; ق ب ل 29:3,18,48; د خ ل 29:9; ف و ق 29:55; ظ ل م 29:14,31,40,46,49,68; ك ذ ب 29:3,12,18,37,68; ي س ر 29:19.
- Synthesis: Provision is materialized as timed husbandry rather than stored possession: access, return, pause, and renewed yield must remain in balance. The scene also usefully pressures the surah's moral vocabulary. ظلم becomes a necessary act performed out of place or season, while كذب becomes milk that seemed dependable but failed; measured dependence therefore clarifies both apportioned provision and the falsity of treating yield as self-renewing.

### S19. Whole-surah (29:1-69) Tanning a Hide by Measured Treatment
- Reading type: latent/lexical
- Scene or process: A hide is removed with hair still attached, its outer surface is pared, tanning bark is applied in measured doses, and the treated material is pierced for use.
- Active motifs: stripping the outer skin from a surface `quranic:root_000120:B004/m01`; raw hide retaining hair through skinning or tanning `quranic:root_000844:B006/m01`; salam bark used as tannin `quranic:root_000737:B008/m01`; one or two measured applications of tanning agent `quranic:root_001533:B007/m01`; piercing and cutting through the treated material `quranic:root_000273:B001/m01`
- Ayah anchors: ب ش ر 29:31; ص ح ب 29:15; س ل م 29:46; ن ف س 29:6,40,57; ج و ب 29:24,29.
- Synthesis: The hide becomes usable through penetration and measured transformation, not through a decorative coating. This usefully pressures the surah's primary contrast between tested truth and fabricated appearance: a merely presented surface can deceive, whereas durable change requires removal, treatment, and an alteration that reaches the material itself.

### S20. Whole-surah (29:1-69) The Grave as the Dead Person's House
- Reading type: latent/lexical
- Scene or process: The dead body is wrapped, concealed in the ground, installed in a grave understood as a house, and left in a prolonged state of residence.
- Active motifs: shroud covering the dead `quranic:root_000266:B009/m01`; burial concealing the body `quranic:root_000266:B009/m02`; grave as the dead person's house `quranic:root_000166:B007/m01`; prolonged residence that can describe the slain `quranic:root_000211:B001/m01`
- Ayah anchors: ج ن ن 29:58; ب ي ت 29:41; ث و ي 29:68.
- Synthesis: Death acquires a dwelling sequence: covering, concealment, installation, and continued residence. This materializes the surah's statement that every self tastes death and sharpens its comparison of dwellings: the fragile worldly house, the grave-house that holds the body, and the prepared upper chambers of the lasting return.

## Cross-Pericope and Whole-Surah Channels

### X1. Nontransferable Liability and Entrusted Provision
- Pericopes: P1 (29:1-13) and P5 (29:57-69).
- Bridge: repeated scene signature with contrast and role reversal; both scenes contain a load, bearer, guarantor, transfer claim, and settlement, but they impose opposite transfer rules.
- Canonical placements: P1 Parent 2 / Subchannel A, "Load, Surety, and Inseparable Liability"; P5 Parent 19 / Subchannel A, "Load, Guarantor, and Entrusted Care".
- Synthesis: Moral liability cannot be detached from the actor, so a promised substitute only acquires an additional burden. Material provision, by contrast, is precisely what a creature unable to carry its future must entrust to a sufficient guardian. The bridge distinguishes evasion from reliance by asking whether the load is constitutive responsibility or a need properly placed in care.

### X2. Allegiance Reversed by Crisis and Safety
- Pericopes: P2 (29:14-27) and P5 (29:57-69).
- Bridge: contrast and reversal; the same roles of worshiper, binding object, allegiance, crisis, and repudiation recur with the direction of change inverted.
- Canonical placements: P2 Parent 4 / Subchannels B-C, "Affection as an Alliance Contract" and "Repudiation, Separation, and Expulsion"; P5 Parent 20 / Subchannels A-B, "Unstable Vessel and the Purified Call" and "Dry-Ground Rescue and Renewed Division".
- Synthesis: In P2 a stable cultic alliance is built through affection and later reverses into mutual disavowal. In P5 danger first strips divided allegiance down to one addressee, while restored safety permits partnership and denial to return. Read together, crisis does not mechanically produce fidelity; it exposes the bond's direction, and the post-crisis act reveals whether purification became durable allegiance.

### X3. Rescue as Separation and Relocation
- Pericopes: P2 (29:14-27) and P3 (29:28-44).
- Bridge: repeated scene signature and causal sequence; an enclosing threat is followed by an external rescuer, separation from danger, rapid movement, and relocation beyond the threatened field.
- Canonical placements: P2 Parent 7 / Subchannel B, "Extraction to Flood-Safe Height"; P2 S6, "Fire Attack and Extractive Rescue"; P3 S8, "Guests, Provision, and Extraction"; P3 S9, "Constricted Reach Opened by Rescue".
- Synthesis: Flood, fire, a hostile town, and constricted personal reach differ as threat media, but rescue keeps the same operation: it does not merely cancel harm in place; it opens an exit and changes the endangered party's location. The rescuing role therefore progresses from preserving companions to extracting a target and finally enabling a household whose own reach has failed.

### X4. Route Agency From Following to Guided Striving
- Pericopes: P1 (29:1-13), P2 (29:14-27), P3 (29:28-44), P4 (29:45-56), and P5 (29:57-69).
- Bridge: repeated scene signature with role progression; traveler, route, directional authority, obstruction or difficult terrain, and terminus recur as follower, migrant, blocked traveler, displaced servant, and guided striver.
- Canonical placements: P1 Parent 3 / Subchannel A, "Worked Track, Foot Travelers, and Road Gear"; P2 S5, "The Migrant as Stranger in Habitable Terrain"; P3 Parent 9 / Subchannel A, "Cutting the Worked Road"; P4 S14, "Spacious Earth as Ground for Service"; P5 Parent 23 / Subchannels A-B, "Exertion Until the Route Appears" and "Hard Ground, Mountain Center, and Supported Gait".
- Synthesis: The surah's road sequence develops moral agency through changing travel conditions. A path can recruit passive following, migration can sever inherited location, hostile power can block public passage, breadth can restore room for service, and exertion can culminate in disclosed routes. Guidance is thus neither bare direction nor frictionless motion; it is the restoration of traversable agency under pressure.

### X5. From Assay Mark to Inward Testimony
- Pericopes: P1 (29:1-13), P2 (29:14-27), P3 (29:28-44), and P4 (29:45-56).
- Bridge: causal sequence and role progression; pressure leaves a distinguishing mark, the mark becomes a visible sign, inspection converts it into an intelligible model, and learned observers retain it as testimony.
- Canonical placements: P1 Parent 1 / Subchannel A, "Fire-Assay and the Mark of Sound Material"; P2 Parent 5 / Subchannel B, "Sign, Banner, and Deliberate Inspection"; P3 Parent 12 / Subchannels A-B, "Mark, Landmark, and Inward Sight" and "Struck Model and Rational Restraint"; P4 Parent 13 / Subchannel C, "Signs Archived in Breasts".
- Synthesis: Evidence progresses through material, spatial, cognitive, and testimonial roles without changing its invariant function of distinction. What pressure first marks can later guide attention, discipline comparison, enter knowledge, and be produced as witness. The whole sequence explains why seeing alone is insufficient: a sign completes its work only when its distinction survives into judgment and testimony.

### X6. Return Through Limit, Re-creation, and Revived Life
- Pericopes: P1 (29:1-13), P2 (29:14-27), and P5 (29:57-69).
- Bridge: shared invariant and causal sequence; departure or loss reaches a limit, reverses direction, and ends in encounter, renewed emergence, or restored life.
- Canonical placements: P1 Parent 3 / Subchannel B, "Race, Finish, and Unavoidable Reception"; P2 Parent 6 / Subchannel B, "Reversal, Return, and Renewed Emergence"; P5 Parent 17 / Subchannels A-B, "Life-Bearing Self, Death, and Return" and "Descending Water and Revived Ground".
- Synthesis: P1 gives return a terminus and face-to-face encounter; P2 gives it the mechanics of turning a made form back into emergence; P5 applies the same sequence to the dead self and exhausted ground. Return is therefore not repetition without change. It is a directed passage through a real limit whose outcome is presence in a renewed state.

### X7. From Interior Claim to Attributed Public Record
- Pericopes: P1 (29:1-13), P2 (29:14-27), P3 (29:28-44), P4 (29:45-56), and P5 (29:57-69).
- Bridge: causal sequence and role progression; an inward source becomes utterance, dispatch, public circulation, ordered inscription, remembered testimony, and finally an attribution that can be tested as true or fabricated.
- Canonical placements: P1 Parent 1 / Subchannels B-C, "Spoken Profession and the Unvoiced Interior" and "Injury Amplified as Public Circulation"; P2 Parent 5 / Subchannel A, "Dispatch, Carriage, and Clear Delivery"; P3 Parent 9 / Subchannel B, "The Abusive Public Council"; P4 Parent 13 / Subchannels A-B, "Hidden Delivery and Durable Inscription" and "Following Voice and Recovered Presence"; P4 S12, "Provenance by Line, Hand, and False Attribution"; P5 Parent 22 / Subchannel B, "Turning Away From the Proper Fit".
- Synthesis: The communication chain begins before speech and ends after reception. Intention supplies provenance; delivery gives the claim a carrier; public repetition can clarify or distort; inscription and remembrance stabilize it; line, hand, testimony, and fit test its attribution. The bridge makes truth a property of the whole chain rather than of fluent wording alone.

## Additional Standalone Subchannels

### S21. Whole-surah (29:1-69) Consecrated Approach and Marked Offering
- Reading type: latent/lexical
- Scene or process: A pilgrim intentionally approaches the sanctuary, enters a state of consecration, assumes its distinctive garment and abstentions, marks an offering, brings it to the precinct, and circles the sacred center.
- Active motifs: intentional approach toward a sacred destination `quranic:root_000053:B012/m01`; entering consecration and accepting ritual abstentions `quranic:root_000313:B004/m01`; consecrated pilgrim's garment `quranic:root_000313:B010/m01`; ritual sign and marked sacrificial animal `quranic:root_000798:B006/m01`; offering brought to the sanctuary `quranic:root_001583:B005/m01`; circumambulation around the house or another center `quranic:root_000957:B001/m01`
- Ayah anchors: ء م م 29:18; ح ر م 29:67; ش ع ر 29:53; ه د ي 29:69; ط و ف 29:14.
- Synthesis: The scene reframes the sanctuary from a static protected perimeter into a destination whose approach changes conduct. Intention, self-limitation, visible marking, relinquished property, and repeated centered movement make guidance and security enacted relations rather than possessions acquired merely by standing inside a boundary.

### S22. Whole-surah (29:1-69) Raising Water From a Newly Dug Well
- Reading type: latent/lexical
- Scene or process: A new well is worked, a bucket is lowered on a long rope, filled, balanced by its loop and handle, and raised through a rotating lifting apparatus so that the water can irrigate land.
- Active motifs: newly dug well `quranic:root_000090:B008/m01`; long, tightly twisted bucket rope `quranic:root_000796:B002/m01`; water-carrier's bucket with one loop `quranic:root_000737:B011/m01`; bucket handle or sidepiece that balances the load `quranic:root_000741:B008/m01`; bucket filled nearly to the brim `quranic:root_000521:B007/m01`; windlass used to draw water `quranic:root_000373:B016/m01`; wheel or timber apparatus for lifting water `quranic:root_000987:B003/m01`; watering land by lifting water with bucket and sāniya `quranic:root_000751:B001/m01`
- Ayah anchors: ب د ء 29:19-20; ش ط ن 29:38; س ل م 29:46; س م ع 29:5,60; ذ ن ب 29:40; ح و ل 29:67; ع ج ل 29:53-54; س ن و 29:14.
- Synthesis: This materializes the surah's provision-and-revival reading at the point where hidden water becomes reachable. Source, rope, container, grip, rotation, and lift must remain functionally aligned; abundance below the surface is not yet received provision. The scene therefore pressures an effortless reading of sustenance while preserving the given source on which the whole apparatus depends.

### S23. Whole-surah (29:1-69) Date Ripening and Palm-Leaf Collection
- Reading type: latent/lexical
- Scene or process: An unripe date reddens, ripening advances from its tip, the ripe fruit breaks free of its peel, begins to dry, and is gathered in a woven palm-leaf basket.
- Active motifs: unripe date turning red `quranic:root_001248:B015/m01`; ripening beginning at the fruit's tip `quranic:root_000521:B005/m01`; ripe date breaking free from its peel `quranic:root_001156:B002/m01`; ripe fruit beginning to dry `quranic:root_001684:B016/m01`; woven palm-leaf basket for dates `quranic:root_000464:B010/m01`
- Ayah anchors: ق ل ب 29:21; ذ ن ب 29:40; ف س ق 29:34; و ل ي 29:22,41; د خ ل 29:9.
- Synthesis: Revived ground reaches provision through staged maturation rather than through instantaneous yield. Color change, localized ripening, release from the peel, drying, and collection turn created growth into food that can be received and kept. The scene thus materializes 29:60-63 and pressures any reading of provision that omits time, transformation, and a fitting receptacle.

### S24. Whole-surah (29:1-69) Clarifying a Pot Over Heat
- Reading type: latent/lexical
- Scene or process: Food is heated in a measured vessel, its harsh force is tempered, fat renders and rises, mixed matter is clarified, a thickened condiment restores consistency, and a cloth permits the hot pot to be removed.
- Active motifs: cooking pot, broth, and the work done in it `quranic:root_001205:B007/m01`; rendered fat or oil rising over a pot `quranic:root_000064:B006/m01`; cooking that diminishes an ingredient's harsh force `quranic:root_001454:B002/m01`; clarifying butter or food from mixed residue `quranic:root_000430:B008/m01`; thickened condiment used to repair or season a preparation `quranic:root_000532:B006/m01`; cloth used to lower a hot pot from the fire `quranic:root_000248:B007/m01`
- Ayah anchors: ق د ر 29:20,62; ء ه ل 29:31-34,46; م و ت 29:57,63; خ ل ص 29:65; ر ب ب 29:10,26,30,50,59; ج ع ل 29:10,15,27,67.
- Synthesis: The domestic process materializes the surah's conjunction of measured provision and purified allegiance. Measure contains the transformation; heat exposes and tempers; clarification separates what had been mixed; repair produces a coherent food rather than a merely altered surface. Purity is consequently an achieved material condition, not a verbal claim made before pressure.

### S25. Whole-surah (29:1-69) Capital Entrusted to a Traveling Trader
- Reading type: latent/lexical
- Scene or process: An owner advances capital to a traveling trader under a profit-sharing agreement; the market opens, goods circulate and stocks diminish, and the venture can end in gain or loss of principal.
- Active motifs: travel-based profit-sharing partnership `quranic:root_000906:B016/m01`; marketplace `quranic:root_000885:B011/m01`; market becoming active and saleable `quranic:root_001273:B018/m01`; brisk sale driven by many buyers `quranic:root_001537:B001/m01`; resulting depletion of stock or capital `quranic:root_001537:B001/m02`; loss of capital or failure to profit `quranic:root_000409:B002/m01`
- Ayah anchors: ض ر ب 29:43; ص ن ع 29:45; ق و م 29:13-14,16,24-25,28-30,35-36,45,51; ن ف ق 29:11; خ س ر 29:52.
- Synthesis: This commercial practice gives 29:12-13 a precise countermodel. Capital, labor, risk, and profit can be contractually entrusted or shared, but a moral burden cannot be transferred by declaring it someone else's account. The scene also materializes striving and provision as exposed travel and uncertain return rather than possession without risk.

### S26. Whole-surah (29:1-69) Convalescent Return
- Reading type: latent/lexical
- Scene or process: After wasting illness, a patient's body turns toward a better condition, gradually approaches its former state, and regains consciousness, strength, and relief.
- Active motifs: body returning to a better condition after wasting or illness `quranic:root_000544:B014/m01`; patient improving and approaching a former healthy state `quranic:root_001397:B012/m01`; consciousness, strength, and respite returning after illness `quranic:root_001188:B005/m01`
- Ayah anchors: ر ج ع 29:8,17,57; م ث ل 29:41,43; ف و ق 29:55.
- Synthesis: Convalescence reframes return and re-creation as restoration of capacity after real depletion, not simple spatial reversal. It materializes the surah's repeated return sequence through a body that remains vulnerable during the interval yet can recover recognizable function and presence.
